### Locked-in §10 paragraph (post-§6tt, eighth revision — SUPERSEDED by post-§6yy above)

This paragraph supersedes the post-§6hh paragraph by adding the
subject-stratification findings that resolve the Reviewer-I
"subject-mix" concern at both the full-grid and hard-subset levels:

- **Subject-stratified WHO ratio (§6rr, full grid):** SS_subject =
  72.1%, SS_helper = 3.3%, ratio = 21.7× (vs §6m primary/helper
  22.1×). The "row factor dominates helper factor" finding survives
  switching the row factor from primary identity (5 levels) to
  subject identity (17 MMLU subjects, n≥5).

- **Subject-stratified WHO ratio (§6ss, hard subset):** SS_subject =
  80.2%, SS_helper = 1.4%, ratio = 56.5× — STRENGTHENS, not dilutes,
  the §6cc hard primary/helper ratio (50.3×). Helper variance shrinks
  to its smallest in the audit (1.4%) on the hard subset.

- **Subject decomposition of mutual-unrecoverability (§6qq):** Within
  math primary, mutually-unrecoverable rate spans 20% (elementary_math)
  to 75% (high_school_math + college_math). The within-primary subject
  heterogeneity is real at the per-cell level — but does not
  propagate to the variance-decomposition headline (per §6rr/§6ss).

- **Per-subject hard W2C bootstrap CI (§6tt):** hs_biology W2C CI
  [50.8, 81.8] does not overlap college_math CI [0.0, 12.5] — the
  cleanest 39 pp non-overlap is the strongest robust subject-level
  contrast. 20 of 136 subject-pair tests pass BH-FDR α=0.05; 0 pass
  Bonferroni-136 due to bootstrap precision floor (1/2001 > α/136).

- **Mutual-unrecoverability mechanism (§6pp):** 47.7% of hard
  questions are nearly unrecoverable (≤1 of 6 helpers help); 38.6%
  mutually unrecoverable (0/6 help). Math/law primaries: 53% mutually
  unrecoverable; biology: 19%.

> **WHO-asymmetry headline (audit §6m, §6rr, §6cc, §6ss):** On the
> verified Qwen3-1.7B LoRA pair-grid (5 primary × 6 helper × 50
> question = 1500 paired observations), the variance-decomposition
> ratio (row factor / helper) is **22.1×** under primary-stratification
> (5 levels) and **21.7×** under subject-stratification (17 MMLU
> subjects, n≥5) — essentially identical. On the n=176 hard-question
> subset (primary solo wrong), the ratio rises to **50.3× primary**
> and **56.5× subject**. Helper variance is 3-4% on the full grid
> and 1.4-1.7% on the hard subset. The "row factor dominates helper
> factor" finding is robust to (a) the choice of row factor (primary
> or subject), (b) restriction to the hard subset, and (c) replication
> across 25 statistical lenses.
>
> **Statistical strength (audit §6r, §6dd, §6w, §6ff, §6tt):**
> Replicate-aware two-way ANOVA on the full grid: F_primary(4, 1470)
> = 26.55, **p < 1e-21**, Cohen's f = 0.27 (medium); F_helper = 0.96,
> p = 0.44, f = 0.06 (trivial). On hard: F_primary = 31.22, **p <
> 1e-23**, f = 0.35; helper F = 0.50. Per-subject hard W2C bootstrap
> (§6tt): 20/136 pairs pass BH-FDR; hs_biology vs college_math
> Δ = +62.5 pp, 95% CI [+44.7, +79.5], p ≈ 0.0005 (precision floor).
>
> **Mechanism (audit §6hh, §6pp, §6qq):** Conversation outcome is
> determined by *the primary's willingness to update from wrong*,
> not by helper identity. Per-primary mean W2C rate (recovery from
> solo wrong) ranges from 21% (math) to 64% (biology); per-helper
> mean W2C rate is essentially flat at 35-41%. Net corrector score
> (W2C − C2W) per primary: biology +51.7 pp, physics +17.9 pp,
> medicine +16.7 pp, math +3.2 pp, law −8.1 pp. **Law primary is
> the only net-distractor row.** 47.7% of hard questions are nearly
> unrecoverable (≤1 of 6 helpers help); biology has the lowest
> mutually-unrecoverable rate (19%) and math/law the highest (53%).
>
> **Subject-level robustness (audit §6tt):** The subject hierarchy
> is robust under bootstrap: hs_biology W2C CI [50.8, 81.8] (n=22),
> astronomy [31, 88] (n=7), college_biology [25.9, 87] (n=9),
> professional_medicine [28.6, 81] (n=7) at the top; college_math
> [0, 12.5] (n=4), hs_math [0, 22.2] (n=12), professional_law
> [10.4, 27.6] (n=32) at the bottom. The 39 pp non-overlap between
> hs_biology and college_math is the cleanest contrast in the audit.
>
> **Cell-level helper roles (audit §6bb):** 24/30 cells (80%) are
> corrector cells (w2c > c2w). All 6 non-corrector cells (5
> distractor + 1 neutral) are in the law primary's row. There is
> not a single helper under which law becomes a corrector cell.
>
> **Non-parametric corroboration (audit §6t, §6u):** Cluster-
> respecting permutation tests give p < 0.0001 for primary
> (within-column shuffle) and p = 0.65 for helper (within-row
> shuffle).
>
> **No primary × helper interaction (audit §6r, §6z):** F = 0.82,
> p = 0.69, f = 0.11. Per-cell additive-model residual testing
> shows 0/30 cells deviate at any correction.
>
> **Roster sensitivity (audit §6c, §6y, §6gg):** Full-grid LOO
> range 10.37–31.33×; hard-only LOO range 15.58–177.14×. Every LOO
> replicate firmly exceeds the no-effect baseline.
>
> **Per-question helper analysis (audit §6s, §6v):** Helper
> correctness on the primary's question is uncorrelated with the
> helper's own-domain self-solo (Spearman ρ = +0.06,
> indistinguishable from chance) — helper structure is absent at
> per-question resolution.
>
> **Oracle ceiling + orchestration (audit §6x, §6aa, §6mm, §6nn):**
> Pooled mean accuracy is 51.0%; best-of-6 oracle 72.4% (+21.4 pp
> gap). In-sample best-by-col-mean closes 33% (+7.0 pp) but LOO-CV
> only 8% (+1.8 pp); easy 30% gap closure, hard 4% — orchestration
> helps preserve correct answers more than recover wrong. Cross-
> domain helpers preferred over domain-matched in 4 of 5 primaries.

stratification level: 3.3% SS on full grid (§6rr), 1.4% on hard
(§6ss), Cohen's f = 0.06–0.13 across subsets, 0/15 helper-pair
Bonferroni survivors and 12/15 BH-FDR-non-survivors (§6jj).

### Locked-in §10 paragraph (post-§6hh, seventh revision — SUPERSEDED by post-§6tt above)

This paragraph supersedes the post-§6cc paragraph by adding the
hard-only inferential confirmations and the recovery-rate mechanistic
framing:

- **Hard-only ANOVA (§6dd):** F_primary = 31.22, p < 1e-23
  (vs full grid F = 26.55, ratio 1.18×). The within-cell noise
  bound (87.8% of hard SS) means the relative F-stat lift is
  modest, but absolute statistical strength is even greater than
  the full-grid baseline.

- **Difficulty-stratified bootstrap (§6ee):** Easy WHO 95% CI
  [0.23, 8.36] (CI includes 1.0); hard WHO 95% CI [10.80, 198.14]
  (CI excludes 1.0 by ~10×); P(hard > easy) = 99.9%.

- **Hard-only effect sizes (§6ff):** Primary Cohen's f lifts from
  0.27 (medium) to 0.35 (medium edging large); ω² from 6.4% to
  10.3% (+60% relative).

- **Hard-only specialist-jackknife (§6gg):** LOO range
  15.58–177.14× — wider than full grid (10.37–31.33×) but firmly
  excludes 1.0 in all 5 LOO replicates.

- **Conditional rates by difficulty (§6hh):** Per-primary mean
  recovery rate (W2C on hard) ranges from 20.8% (math) to 64.0%
  (biology); per-helper W2C is 35-41% across all 6 (flat).
  Primary spread / helper spread on W2C = 7.07×. Net corrector
  scores: biology +52, physics +18, medicine +17, math +3, law −8.

> **Headline statistical claim (audit §6r, §6dd, §6w, §6ff):** On
> the verified Qwen3-1.7B LoRA pair-grid (5 primary × 6 helper × 50
> question = 1500 paired observations), replicate-aware two-way
> ANOVA attributes 6.6% of total variance to primary identity
> (F(4, 1470) = 26.55, **p < 1e-21**, Cohen's f = 0.27 medium,
> ω² = 6.4%) and 0.3% to helper identity (F = 0.96, p = 0.44,
> f = 0.06 trivial). Restricted to the n=176 hard-question
> subset (primary solo wrong), the primary effect strengthens to
> F = 31.22 (**p < 1e-23**, f = 0.35, ω² = 10.3%); the helper
> effect weakens further (F = 0.50, f = 0.05).
>
> **Mechanism (audit §6hh):** Conversation outcome is determined
> by *the primary's willingness to update from wrong*, not by
> helper identity. Per-primary mean W2C rate (recovery from solo
> wrong) ranges from 20.8% (math) to 64.0% (biology); per-helper
> mean W2C rate is essentially flat at 35-41% across all 6
> helpers. Net corrector score (W2C − C2W) per primary: biology
> +51.7 pp, physics +17.9 pp, medicine +16.7 pp, math +3.2 pp,
> law −8.1 pp. **Law primary is the only net-distractor row in
> the grid.**
>
> **Cell-level helper roles (audit §6bb):** 24/30 cells (80%) are
> corrector cells (w2c > c2w). All 6 non-corrector cells
> (5 distractor + 1 neutral) are in the law primary's row. There
> is *not a single helper* under which law becomes a corrector
> cell. The four other primaries are 6/6 corrector each.
>
> **Variance decomposition / WHO ratio (audit §6cc, §6ee, §6hh):**
> The cell-mean WHO ratio (SS_primary / SS_helper) is 22.1× pooled
> (95% CI [5.78, 66.05]); 67.69× on hard (95% CI [10.80, 198.14])
> with primary explaining 88.4% of cell-mean variance; 1.50× on
> easy (CI includes 1.0). The hard-question primary effect has
> a 7.07× advantage over helper effect in conditional W2C rates.
>
> **Non-parametric corroboration (audit §6t, §6u):** Cluster-
> respecting permutation tests give p < 0.0001 for primary
> (within-column shuffle) and p = 0.65 for helper (within-row
> shuffle).
>
> **No primary × helper interaction (audit §6r, §6z):** F = 0.82,
> p = 0.69, f = 0.11. Per-cell additive-model residual testing
> (§6z) shows 0/30 cells deviate at any correction (uncorrected,
> Bonferroni, BH-FDR).
>
> **Roster sensitivity + specialist-jackknife (audit §6c, §6y, §6gg):**
> Full-grid LOO range 10.37–31.33×; hard-only LOO range
> 15.58–177.14×. The law specialist (a CaseHOLD format-matcher)
> has the largest leverage on the full grid (Δ = −11.74); on the
> hard subset it amplifies to Δ = −48.57 but the medicine row
> takes the new max-leverage spot (Δ = +109.45 attenuation).
> No LOO replicate produces a hard ratio below 15× or full ratio
> below 10×.
>
> **Per-question helper analysis (audit §6s, §6v):** 49.2% of
> questions show helpers disagree on correctness, but the
> disagreement is unstructured: pooled Spearman ρ between helper
> correctness and helper own-domain self-solo is +0.064
> (P(ρ > 0) = 49.6%, indistinguishable from chance).
>
> **Oracle ceiling + orchestration (audit §6x, §6aa):** Pooled
> mean accuracy across helpers is 51.0%; best-of-6 oracle 72.4%
> (+21.4 pp gap). The "best-by-col-mean per primary" rule closes
> 33% of the gap (+7.0 pp); the optimal helper is **cross-domain
> for 4 of 5 primaries** (only math primary best with base;
> medicine and biology best with law specialist; law with
> medicine; physics with biology). Self-match is never optimal.

### Defensible one-paragraph abstract sentence (post-§6hh)

The single sentence the paper abstract should use:

> *On the verified Qwen3-1.7B LoRA pair-grid (5 primary × 6 helper
> × 50 question = 1500 paired observations), replicate-aware
> two-way ANOVA attributes 6.6% of variance to primary identity
> (F(4, 1470) = 26.55, p < 1e-21, Cohen's f = 0.27, ω² = 6.4%)
> and 0.3% to helper identity (F = 0.96, p = 0.44, f = 0.06).
> Restricted to hard questions (primary solo wrong), the primary
> effect strengthens (F = 31.22, p < 1e-23, f = 0.35, ω² = 10.3%)
> while the helper effect weakens further. Mechanistically, the
> per-primary mean recovery rate from wrong (W2C) ranges from
> 21% (math) to 64% (biology), while per-helper W2C is flat at
> 35-41%; the primary's spread on recovery is 7.07× the helper's
> spread. 24 of 30 cells are corrector cells, with all 6
> non-corrector cells in the law primary's row. **Conversation
> outcome is determined by the primary's willingness to update
> from wrong, not by helper identity** — a per-primary
> best-helper rule closes 33% of a +21.4 pp oracle ceiling, with
> cross-domain helpers preferred over domain-matched helpers in
> 4 of 5 primaries.*

This is the canonical sentence (post-§6hh). The paper abstract
should derive from it; do not re-write.

### Locked-in §10 paragraph (post-§6cc, sixth revision — SUPERSEDED by post-§6hh above)

This paragraph supersedes the post-§6aa paragraph by adding two new
findings that materially refine the headline interpretation:

- **Helper-as-corrector cell role (§6bb):** 24/30 cells (80%) are
  CORRECTOR cells (helper net-pulled primary toward correct answers,
  w2c > c2w). The 5 distractor cells and the 1 neutral cell are
  *all* in the law primary's row (0/6 corrector for law); the four
  other primaries (math, medicine, biology, physics) have 6/6
  corrector cells each. Every helper has the same 4/5
  corrector-vs-1/5 distractor-or-neutral pattern, with the
  non-corrector cell always being law-primary. **The "rank-
  constraint destroys collaboration" framing is sustained only
  conditionally on law-as-primary**; it does not generalize.

- **Difficulty stratification (§6cc):** The pooled WHO ratio of
  22.11× decomposes into 1.50× on easy questions (n=74, primary
  solo correct, primary effect 24% of variance) and 67.69× on hard
  questions (n=176, primary solo wrong, primary effect 88.4% of
  variance). The WHO-asymmetry is concentrated on hard questions —
  exactly the questions where collaboration matters most.

> **WHO-asymmetry headline (replicate-aware ANOVA, audit §6r):** On
> the verified Qwen3-1.7B LoRA pair-grid (5 primary × 6 helper × 50
> question = 1500 paired observations), two-way ANOVA with replicates
> attributes 6.6% of total variance to primary identity (F(4, 1470)
> = 26.55, **p < 1e-10**) and 0.3% to helper identity
> (F = 0.96, p = 0.44); within-cell question-level noise is 92.0%
> of total variance. **Only the primary main effect is statistically
> significant.**
>
> **Difficulty stratification (audit §6cc):** Splitting the per_q
> records by primary solo correctness, the WHO ratio is **67.69× on
> hard questions** (n=176; primary explains 88.4% of cell-mean
> variance) and **1.50× on easy questions** (n=74; primary 24%,
> helper 16%, comparable). The pooled 22.11× headline is a weighted
> average of these two regimes; the asymmetry is concentrated where
> primary identity matters most — on questions the primary would
> otherwise get wrong.
>
> **Cell-level helper roles (audit §6bb):** Of 30 cells, 24 (80%)
> are corrector cells (w2c > c2w), 5 are distractor (c2w > w2c),
> and 1 is neutral. **All 6 non-corrector cells are in the law
> primary's row.** The four other primaries' rows are universally
> corrector. Every helper (base + 5 specialists) has 4/5 corrector
> + 1/5 non-corrector cells, with the non-corrector cell always
> being law-primary. There is *not a single helper* under which
> law becomes a corrector cell.
>
> **Effect-size standardization (audit §6w):** Cohen's f = 0.269
> places the primary effect in the "medium" range (Cohen 1988);
> the helper main effect (f = 0.057) is below the "small" threshold
> of 0.10. Bias-corrected ω² estimates are 6.4% (primary) and 0.0%
> (helper).
>
> **Non-parametric corroboration (audit §6t, §6u):** Cluster-
> respecting permutation tests (within-row shuffle preserving the
> primary's question pool) give p = 0.65 for the helper effect and
> p < 0.0001 for the primary effect (within-column shuffle).
> Strict-shuffle null gives p = 0.001 (combined). Four converging
> tests reject H0 of no primary effect; four helper-resolution tests
> fail to reject H0 of no helper effect.
>
> **No primary × helper interaction (audit §6r, §6z):** The ANOVA
> interaction term is F(20, 1470) = 0.82 (p = 0.69), Cohen's
> f = 0.106 (small), ω² = 0.000. Per-cell residual testing against
> the additive-model prediction (audit §6z) shows 0/30 cells
> deviate at uncorrected p < 0.05, Bonferroni p < 0.05/30, or
> BH-FDR q = 0.05.
>
> **Subject-stratification (audit §6q):** Re-aggregating per
> (primary, subject, helper) on the 19-subject grid drops
> rows-of-total variance from 83.3% (5×6) to 65.2%; hierarchical
> decomposition attributes 43.4% of total variance to between-
> primary differences and 21.8% to within-primary subject mix.
>
> **Bootstrap CIs on the descriptive cell-mean ratio (audit §6f):**
> Cell-mean spread ratio = 4.47×, question-clustered 95% CI
> [2.20, 7.45]; cell-mean variance ratio = 22.1×, 95% CI
> [5.78, 66.05].
>
> **Roster sensitivity + specialist-jackknife (audit §6c, §6y, §6bb):**
> The law specialist (a CaseHOLD format-matcher; audit §1) carries
> the largest leverage on every metric: jackknife Δ = −11.74 on the
> WHO ratio (§6y); 5/6 distractor cells in §6bb; 0/6 significant
> cells in §6o. **Without the law primary's row, the WHO ratio
> drops from 22.11× to 10.37×** but remains > 5×. The honest claim
> is therefore "the WHO-asymmetry is real and large; its magnitude
> is law-amplified" — not "law alone causes it."
>
> **Per-cell power audit (audit §6o):** Of 30 cells, 17 have a
> Wilson 95% CI on their delta-vs-solo that excludes zero. Biology
> and physics rows are 6/6 each; medicine 5/6; math and law 0/6
> each.
>
> **Per-question helper analysis (audit §6s, §6v):** At the per-
> question level, 49.2% of questions show helpers disagree on
> correctness, but the disagreement is *unstructured* by helper
> competence: pooled Spearman ρ between helper correctness and
> helper own-domain self-solo is +0.064 (P(ρ > 0) = 49.6%).
>
> **Oracle ceiling + orchestration (audit §6x, §6aa):** Pooled
> mean accuracy across helpers is 51.0%; best-of-6-helpers oracle
> is **72.4%**, a +21.4 pp gap. The "best-by-col-mean per primary"
> rule closes **33% of the gap** (+7.0 pp lift to 58.0%); the
> best helpers are **cross-domain for 4 of 5 primaries** (only
> math primary's best helper is base; medicine and biology
> primaries best paired with law specialist; law primary with
> medicine; physics primary with biology).

### Defensible one-paragraph abstract sentence (post-§6cc)

The single sentence the paper abstract should use:

> *On the verified Qwen3-1.7B LoRA pair-grid (5 primary × 6 helper
> × 50 question = 1500 paired observations), replicate-aware
> two-way ANOVA attributes 6.6% of total variance to primary
> identity (F(4, 1470) = 26.55, p < 1e-10; Cohen's f = 0.269) and
> 0.3% to helper identity (F = 0.96, p = 0.44; f = 0.057);
> 24 of 30 cells are corrector cells (w2c > c2w) with the 6
> non-corrector cells all concentrated in the law primary's row.
> Stratifying by primary solo correctness, the variance asymmetry
> is concentrated on hard questions (WHO ratio 67.69× on n=176
> primary-wrong questions vs 1.50× on n=74 primary-correct
> questions). Conversation outcome is determined by which
> specialist holds the question — and most strongly so on
> questions it would otherwise get wrong — not by which helper is
> in the room. A per-primary helper-selection rule
> ("best-by-col-mean") closes 33% of the +21.4 pp oracle ceiling,
> with cross-domain helpers preferred over domain-matched helpers
> in 4 of 5 primaries.*

This is the canonical sentence. The paper abstract should derive
from it, not re-write it.

### Triangulation update (post-§6dd)

Adding §6bb, §6cc, and §6dd rows to the converging-tests tables:

| Primary-effect test | Result | Audit section |
|---|---|---|
| Within-cell bootstrap (n=1000) | 95% CI [1.89, 8.39] excludes 1 | §6f |
| Question-clustered bootstrap (n=2000) | 95% CI [2.20, 7.45] excludes 1 | §6f revised |
| Replicate-aware 2-way ANOVA (full) | F(4, 1470) = 26.55, **p < 1e-10** | §6r |
| Cohen's f effect size | f = 0.269 (medium); ω² = 0.064 | §6w |
| Within-column cluster permutation (n=5000) | p < 0.0001 | §6u |

| **Hard-question WHO ratio (n=176)** | **67.69× (88.4% rows)** | **§6cc** |
| **Hard-only replicate-aware ANOVA** | **F(4, 1026) = 31.22, p < 1e-23** | **§6dd** |
| **Difficulty-stratified bootstrap (P[hard > easy])** | **99.9% (1998/2000 iter)** | **§6ee** |

**Ten** converging primary-effect tests now, all rejecting H0. The
§6dd hard-only F = 31.22 is a more conservative inferential check
than the cell-mean §6cc 67.69×; the §6ee bootstrap (P = 99.9% that
hard ratio exceeds easy ratio) confirms the difficulty stratification
is robust under question-level resampling.

The interpretation that survives every test:

- Primary identity drives the outcome (medium effect size, 8/8
  tests significant, 88.4% of variance on hard questions).
- Helper identity has no main effect at any aggregation level
  tested (6/6 helper-resolution tests fail to reject H0).
- No primary × helper interaction (6/6 tests fail to reject H0).
- 80% of cells are corrector cells; the 20% non-corrector cells
  are concentrated in one primary's row (law).
- Per-primary best-helper selection captures 33% of a +21.4 pp
  oracle gap — exploitable but only conditional on primary.

### Locked-in §10 paragraph (post-§6aa, fifth revision — SUPERSEDED by post-§6cc above)

This paragraph supersedes the post-§6v paragraph. Each clause now
points to the supporting audit section, and the paragraph adds three
new findings: effect-size standardization (§6w), oracle ceiling +
specialist-jackknife (§6x/§6y), no interaction structure (§6z), and
the orchestration-predictor positive finding (§6aa).

> **WHO-asymmetry headline (replicate-aware ANOVA, audit §6r):** On
> the verified Qwen3-1.7B LoRA pair-grid (5 primary × 6 helper × 50
> question = 1500 paired observations), two-way ANOVA with replicates
> attributes 6.6% of total variance to primary identity (F(4, 1470)
> = 26.55, **p < 1e-10**) and 0.3% to helper identity
> (F = 0.96, p = 0.44); within-cell question-level noise is 92.0%
> of total variance. **Only the primary main effect is statistically
> significant.**
>
> **Effect-size standardization (audit §6w):** Cohen's f = 0.269
> places the primary effect in the "medium" range (Cohen 1988);
> the helper main effect (f = 0.057) is below the "small" threshold
> of 0.10. Bias-corrected ω² estimates are 6.4% (primary) and 0.0%
> (helper).
>
> **Non-parametric corroboration (audit §6t, §6u):** Cluster-
> respecting permutation tests (within-row shuffle preserving the
> primary's question pool) give p = 0.65 for the helper effect and
> p < 0.0001 for the primary effect (within-column shuffle).
> Strict-shuffle null gives p = 0.001 (combined). Four converging
> tests reject H0 of no primary effect; four helper-resolution tests
> fail to reject H0 of no helper effect.
>
> **No primary × helper interaction (audit §6r, §6z):** The ANOVA
> interaction term is F(20, 1470) = 0.82 (p = 0.69), Cohen's
> f = 0.106 (small), ω² = 0.000. Per-cell residual testing against
> the additive-model prediction (audit §6z) shows 0/30 cells
> deviate at uncorrected p < 0.05, Bonferroni p < 0.05/30, or
> BH-FDR q = 0.05. The §6e flagged "biology helper at −6 pp on
> math primary" outlier is z = −1.51, p = 0.13 (within sampling
> noise). The additive model `(primary + helper)` is sufficient.
>
> **Subject-stratification (audit §6q):** Re-aggregating per
> (primary, subject, helper) on the 19-subject grid drops
> rows-of-total variance from 83.3% (5×6) to 65.2%; hierarchical
> decomposition attributes 43.4% of total variance to between-
> primary differences and 21.8% to within-primary subject mix.
> The honest claim is "**the primary's question-set composition**
> dominates outcome variance" rather than "primary identity dominates."
>
> **Bootstrap CIs on the descriptive cell-mean ratio (audit §6f):**
> Cell-mean spread ratio = 4.47×, question-clustered 95% CI
> [2.20, 7.45]; cell-mean variance ratio = 22.1×, 95% CI
> [5.78, 66.05]. Both descriptive complements to the inferential
> ANOVA above.
>
> **Roster sensitivity + specialist-jackknife (audit §6c, §6y):**
> Roster-sensitivity envelope (drop-one-helper-class) is
> 3.05×–6.27×; specialist leave-one-out envelope is 10.37×–31.33×
> (mean 17.67×, std 8.38). The law specialist (a CaseHOLD format-
> matcher; audit §1) carries the largest leverage (Δ = −11.74);
> dropping it collapses the variance ratio by more than half.
> Both envelopes consistently exclude 1.0.
>
> **Per-cell power audit (audit §6o):** Of 30 cells, 17 have a
> Wilson 95% CI on their delta-vs-solo that excludes zero. Biology
> and physics rows are 6/6 each; medicine 5/6; math and law 0/6
> each. The pooled estimate is well-supported because 12 of 17
> significant cells fall in two strong rows.
>
> **Per-question helper analysis (audit §6s, §6v):** At the per-
> question level, 49.2% of questions show helpers disagree on
> correctness, but the disagreement is *unstructured* by helper
> competence: pooled Spearman ρ between helper correctness and
> helper own-domain self-solo is +0.064 (P(ρ > 0) = 49.6%,
> indistinguishable from chance).
>
> **Oracle ceiling + orchestration (audit §6x, §6aa):** Pooled
> mean accuracy across helpers is 51.0%; best-of-6-helpers oracle
> is **72.4%**, a +21.4 pp gap. Among non-learned predictors
> tested, "best-by-col-mean per primary" closes **33% of the
> gap** (+7.0 pp lift to 58.0%); majority-vote-of-6 closes 20%
> (+4.2 pp); always-base, always-self-match, best-self-solo, and
> subject-aware lookup all underperform random. The best-by-col-
> mean rule's optimal helper is **cross-domain for 4 of 5
> primaries** — only math primary's best helper is base; medicine
> and biology primaries are best paired with law specialist; law
> primary with medicine; physics primary with biology. Self-match
> (primary-as-helper) is *never* the best choice in any primary's
> row.

### Defensible one-paragraph abstract sentence (post-§6aa)

The single sentence the paper abstract should use:

> *On the verified Qwen3-1.7B LoRA pair-grid (5 primary × 6 helper
> × 50 question = 1500 paired observations), replicate-aware
> two-way ANOVA attributes 6.6% of total variance to primary
> identity (F(4, 1470) = 26.55, p < 1e-10; Cohen's f = 0.269,
> "medium") and 0.3% to helper identity (F = 0.96, p = 0.44;
> Cohen's f = 0.057, "trivial"); cluster-respecting permutation,
> question-clustered bootstrap, and per-cell additive-model
> residual testing all converge on the same reading. Conversation
> outcome is determined by which specialist holds the question,
> not by which helper is in the room — although a per-primary
> helper-selection rule ("best-by-col-mean") closes 33% of the
> +21.4 pp oracle ceiling, with cross-domain helpers preferred
> over domain-matched helpers in 4 of 5 primaries.*

### Triangulation tables (post-§6aa)

**Six** converging primary-effect tests (all reject H0):

| Test | Result | Audit section |
|---|---|---|
| Within-cell bootstrap (n=1000) | 95% CI [1.89, 8.39] excludes 1 | §6f |
| Question-clustered bootstrap (n=2000) | 95% CI [2.20, 7.45] excludes 1 | §6f revised |
| Replicate-aware 2-way ANOVA | F(4, 1470) = 26.55, **p < 1e-10** | §6r |
| Cohen's f effect size | f = 0.269 (medium); ω² = 0.064 | §6w |
| Within-column cluster permutation (n=5000) | p < 0.0001 | §6u |
| Specialist-jackknife (5 LOO replicates) | range 10.37–31.33×, all > 1 | §6y |

**Six** converging helper-effect tests (none reject H0):

| Test | Result | Audit section |
|---|---|---|
| Replicate-aware ANOVA helper main | F = 0.96, p = 0.44 | §6r |
| Strict-shuffle (combined w/ primary) | p = 0.001 (joint) | §6t |
| Within-row cluster permutation | p = 0.65 | §6u |
| Cohen's f effect size | f = 0.057 (trivial); ω² = 0.000 | §6w |
| Col-mean Pearson r vs self-solo (n=5) | r = +0.51 (aggregation only) | §6h |
| Per-question Spearman ρ vs self-solo (n=123) | ρ = +0.064, P(ρ > 0) = 49.6% | §6v |

**Six** converging interaction-effect tests (all consistent with no interaction):

| Test | Result | Audit section |
|---|---|---|
| Cell-mean ANOVA SS_residual | 12.9% (mostly within-cell noise per §6r) | §6m |
| Replicate-aware ANOVA interaction | F(20, 1470) = 0.82, p = 0.69 | §6r |
| Cohen's f effect size | f = 0.106 (small); ω² = 0.000 | §6w |
| Tukey-style per-cell residuals (uncorrected) | 0/30 cells significant | §6z |
| Tukey-style per-cell residuals (Bonferroni) | 0/30 cells significant | §6z |
| Tukey-style per-cell residuals (BH-FDR) | 0/30 cells significant | §6z |

**Primary effect: rejected H0 in 6/6 tests.**
**Helper effect: not rejected in 6/6 tests with helper resolution.**
**Interaction effect: not rejected in 6/6 tests.**

The §6x oracle gap (+21.4 pp) and §6aa orchestration finding
(33% gap-closure with cross-domain helpers preferred) are *not*
hypothesis tests; they are *positive* findings that complement the
no-helper-main-effect / no-interaction null. The combined picture:

- **Primary identity dominates** (medium effect size, 6/6 tests
  significant).
- **Helper identity has no main effect** at any aggregation level
  tested.
- **No primary × helper interaction** survives any of 6 corrections.
- **But** per-primary best-helper selection is exploitable: 33% of
  the +21.4 pp oracle gap is closable by a simple rule, and the
  best helpers are cross-domain (medicine ↔ law, biology ↔ law,
  physics ↔ biology) — a paper-worthy positive structural finding.

This is the cleanest statistical story the audit produces. It maps
directly to a paper claim:

> *Conversation outcome is determined by which specialist holds the
> question (primary effect, F = 26.55, p < 1e-10, ω² = 0.064); the
> helper effect is statistically null (F = 0.96, p = 0.44; ρ = +0.064
> per-q) and there is no primary × helper interaction (F = 0.82,
> p = 0.69; 0/30 cells deviate from the additive model). However,
> a +21.4 pp oracle gap exists that simple per-primary helper
> selection (best-by-col-mean) closes 33% of, with cross-domain
> helpers preferred over domain-matched helpers in 4 of 5
> primaries — pointing to a productive helper-orchestration
> research question.*

---

### Locked-in §10 paragraph (post-§6v, fourth revision — SUPERSEDED by post-§6aa above)

This paragraph is the audit's recommended headline statistical
framing for the verified Qwen3-1.7B LoRA pair-grid. Each clause
points to the audit section that supports it:

> **WHO-asymmetry headline (replicate-aware ANOVA, audit §6r):** On
> the verified Qwen3-1.7B LoRA pair-grid (5 primary × 6 helper × 50
> question = 1500 paired observations Y_ijk = pair_correct[i,j,k] −
> solo_correct[i,k] ∈ {−1, 0, +1}), two-way ANOVA with replicates
> attributes 6.6% of total variance to primary identity (F(4, 1470)
> = 26.55, **p < 1e-10**), 0.3% to helper identity (F = 0.96, p =
> 0.44), 1.0% to primary × helper interaction (F = 0.82, p = 0.69),
> and 92.0% to within-cell question-level noise. **Only the primary
> main effect is statistically significant.** Cell-mean variance
> ratios (e.g. the §6m primary/helper SS ratio of 22.1×) are
> descriptive at the cell-aggregation level; the inferential
> reading is the F-test.
>
> **Non-parametric corroboration (audit §6t, §6u):** Permutation
> tests on the cell-mean WHO ratio confirm the parametric finding.
> A strict-shuffle null (n=5000 iterations) gives p = 0.001 for the
> combined primary+helper structure; decomposing into within-row
> shuffle (preserves question-clustering, tests helper effect) gives
> **p = 0.65** — helpers are exchangeable within primaries — and
> within-column shuffle (tests primary effect) gives **p < 0.0001**.
> Four converging tests reject H0 of no primary effect; two
> helper-resolution tests fail to reject H0 of no helper effect.
>
> **Per-question helper analysis (audit §6s, §6v):** At the per-
> question level, 49.2% of questions show helpers disagree on
> correctness — there is per-question helper-induced variation that
> the cell-mean tests cannot detect. However, the disagreement is
> *unstructured*: pooled Spearman ρ between helper correctness and
> helper own-domain self-solo accuracy across 123 disagree-questions
> is **ρ = +0.064** (P(ρ > 0) = 49.6%, indistinguishable from
> chance). Helper identity contributes per-question noise but no
> per-question signal that tracks helper competence.
>
> **Subject-stratification (audit §6q):** Re-aggregating per
> (primary, subject, helper) on the 19-subject grid drops rows-of-
> total variance from 83.3% (5×6 grid) to 65.2%; a hierarchical
> decomposition attributes 43.4% of total variance to between-
> primary differences and 21.8% to within-primary subject
> heterogeneity. The honest claim is therefore "**the primary's
> question-set composition** dominates outcome variance" rather
> than "primary identity dominates" — a meaningful share of what
> looked like primary-effect is in fact within-primary subject mix.
>
> **Bootstrap CIs on the descriptive cell-mean ratio (audit §6f):**
> The cell-mean spread ratio (canonical §6b aggregator: delta cells,
> full 5×5 + base helper, mean-row vs mean-col) is **4.47×**, with
> question-clustered 95% CI **[2.20, 7.45]** (n=2000 iterations,
> P(ratio > 1) = 100%, P(ratio > 2) = 99.1%). The cell-mean
> variance ratio (§6m) is **22.1×** with 95% CI [5.78, 66.05]. The
> two are different statistics on the same data; both should be
> cited as descriptive complements to the inferential ANOVA.
>
> **Roster sensitivity (audit §6c):** The cell-mean spread ratio
> attenuates to 3.05× when the law specialist (CaseHOLD format-
> matcher) is dropped, 3.25× when the roster is restricted to
> specialists whose solo accuracy on the pair-grid eval exceeds
> the base model, and rises to 6.27× when the base helper is
> excluded. **Direction survives all restrictions; magnitude
> envelope is 3.05×–6.27×** with the canonical headline at 4.47×.
>
> **Per-cell power audit (audit §6o):** Only 17 of 30 cells have a
> Wilson 95% CI on their delta-vs-solo that excludes zero. Biology
> and physics primaries are 6/6 significant; medicine is 5/6;
> math and law primaries are 0/6 each. The pooled estimate is
> well-supported because 12 of 17 significant cells fall in two
> strong rows; cell-level claims need explicit n_sig disclosure.

### Defensible one-paragraph abstract sentence

For the paper abstract, this single sentence captures the
locked-in headline:

> *On the verified Qwen3-1.7B LoRA pair-grid (5 primary × 6 helper
> × 50 question = 1500 observations), two-way ANOVA with replicates
> attributes 6.6% of variance to primary identity (F(4, 1470) =
> 26.55, p < 1e-10) and 0.3% to helper identity (F = 0.96, p =
> 0.44); cluster-permutation tests, question-clustered bootstrap CIs
> (4.47× cell-mean spread ratio, 95% CI [2.20, 7.45]; 22.1× cell-mean
> variance ratio), and a per-question helper-correctness analysis
> (pooled Spearman ρ = +0.064 between helper correctness and helper
> self-solo on 123 disagree-questions) all converge on the same
> reading: in this setting, conversation outcome is determined by
> which specialist holds the question, not by which helper is in
> the room.*

### Triangulation tables

The audit now contains *four* converging primary-effect tests:

| Test | Result on primary effect | Audit section |
|---|---|---|
| Within-cell bootstrap (n=1000) | 95% CI [1.89, 8.39] excludes 1 | §6f |
| Question-clustered bootstrap (n=2000) | 95% CI [2.20, 7.45] excludes 1 | §6f revised |
| Replicate-aware 2-way ANOVA | F(4, 1470) = 26.55, p < 1e-10 | §6r |
| Within-column cluster permutation (n=5000) | p < 0.0001 | §6u |

And *five* converging helper-effect tests:

| Test | Result on helper effect | Audit section |
|---|---|---|
| Replicate-aware ANOVA helper main | F = 0.96, p = 0.44 | §6r |
| Strict-shuffle (combined w/ primary) | p = 0.001 | §6t |
| Within-row cluster permutation | p = 0.65 | §6u |
| Col-mean Pearson r vs self-solo (n=5) | r = +0.51 (aggregation only) | §6h |
| Per-question Spearman ρ vs self-solo (n=123) | ρ = +0.064, P > 0 = 49.6% | §6v |

**Primary effect: rejected H0 in 4/4 tests with primary resolution.
Helper effect: not rejected in 4/4 tests with helper resolution
(only the §6t combined test gave p = 0.001, decomposed into
primary-driven by §6u and §6r).**

This is the cleanest statistical convergence the audit has produced.
The paper should lead with the F-test (most precise), back it up
with the cluster permutation (assumption-free), and disclose the
per-question helper analysis (anti-cherry-picking proof).

---

### Earlier-revision draft sentences (preserved for audit trail)

This is the audit's directive to the paper-writing branch. Pasting
sentences that are *defensible* given the verified-roster evidence:

> **Headline (variance framing, after §6m):** On the verified 5×5
> Qwen3-1.7B LoRA pair-grid, two-way variance decomposition of the
> 30 delta cells attributes **83.3% of variance to primary identity**,
> **3.8% to helper identity**, and **12.9% to interaction (residual)**
> — a primary/helper variance ratio of **22.1× (95% CI 5.78–66.05,
> question-clustered bootstrap, P > 5 = 98.9%)**. This says
> conversation outcome is dominated by which agent holds the question,
> not which two adapters are in the room.

> **Headline (spread framing, complementary):** With the primary
> specialist held fixed and the helper varied across five MMLU
> domains plus a base partner, the spread of *gain from collaboration*
> across primaries is **34.0 percentage points**, while the spread
> across helpers is **7.6 pp** — a primary-effect-to-helper-effect
> ratio of **4.47× (95% CI 2.20–7.45, question-clustered)**.

> **Robustness.** The ratio survives every restriction we tested:
> 6.27× when the base helper is excluded, 3.25× when the roster is
> restricted to specialists whose solo accuracy on the pair-grid eval
> exceeds the base model, and 3.05× when the law specialist (a
> CaseHOLD format-matcher) is dropped.

> **Switching mechanics (revised after §6g X-parsing correction).**
> Across 30 condition-cells (5 primaries × 6 helpers, N=50 each) the
> conditional switch rate P(C2W | started correct, letter-only) and
> P(W2C | started wrong, letter-only) are 36.9% and 40.3% respectively,
> giving a letter-only rate ratio of **0.92** — close to balanced, not
> "net-helpful" as the X-inclusive (0.70) ratio suggested.
> 51.6% of pre-collaboration extractions in this protocol are parsing
> failures (special token X), and 67.5% of all W2C events are
> X→letter parsing recoveries rather than genuine peer-induced
> updates. The X-inclusive 0.70 number should be reported only as a
> sub-result with the parsing-recovery share disclosed.

> **Per-primary heterogeneity.** Letter-only rate ratios vary
> 13× across primaries: biology 0.13 (robust net-helpful), medicine
> 0.64, physics 1.24, math 1.54, law 2.04. Three of five primaries
> are net-harmful in letter-only terms; only biology robustly
> remains net-helpful. The pooled "net direction" finding is fragile.

> **Wrong-to-wrong noise.** A quarter of all decisions are
> wrong→wrong switches: agents rapidly cycle between incorrect
> answers under deliberation pressure. We disclose this as a
> separate denominator contribution to the rate-ratio framing.

> **Stickiness and recovery.** Across 30 cells, holding rate is
> uncorrelated with C2W|C (Spearman ρ = +0.08) and strongly
> anti-correlated with W2C|W (ρ = −0.92). LoRA specialists that
> "stick to their guns" abandon the recovery channel without
> preserving correctness; the rank-amplification effect is asymmetric
> in direction, not in magnitude.

> **Pair swap asymmetry.** For 10 cross-domain pairs, the gap
> between pair_X_Y and pair_Y_X averages 20 pp (raw, N=50/cell);
> the biology↔law swap is 44 pp. Conversation outcome is dominated
> by which agent holds the question, not which two adapters are in
> the room.

> **What we cannot yet claim.** The 4B Full FT pair-grid currently
> exists for only 3 of 5 domains and only against the base helper.
> The 1.7B Full FT pair-grid is blocked on training one missing
> specialist (law) and on a matched-solo-accuracy checkpoint
> selection step. Direct LoRA-vs-FT comparison at matched solo
> accuracy — the experiment that would let us say "rank constraint
> destroys collaboration" — has not been run.

> **Subject-mix caveat (after §6n).** Within-primary delta spread
> across MMLU subjects (math 43 pp, law 43 pp) is comparable to
> between-primary row-mean spread (34 pp). The "primary identity
> dominates" framing therefore subsumes a substantial
> subject-mix-within-primary component. The honest line is "the
> *primary's question-set composition* dominates outcome variance",
> with subject-stratification queued as audit follow-up #12.

> **Cell-level power (after §6o).** Of 30 (primary × helper) cells,
> 17 have a Wilson 95% CI on the delta-vs-solo that excludes zero.
> Biology and physics primaries each contribute 6/6 significant
> cells; medicine contributes 5/6; math and law each contribute 0/6.
> The pooled WHO-asymmetry estimate is well-supported because
> 12 of 17 significant cells fall in the two strong rows. Cell-level
> claims need explicit n_sig disclosure; the abstract should not
> cite individual cell deltas without flagging which primaries are
> individually underpowered.

> **Within-helper variance dominates (after §6p).** Helper
> col-means (range 17.2 → 24.8 pp, spread 7.6 pp) sit *inside* the
> within-helper across-primary std (0.09 to 0.21, equivalent to
> ±9–21 pp around each helper's col-mean). Helper-vs-helper
> rankings are therefore meaningful only when conditioned on the
> primary; saying "biology is a worse helper than math (col-mean
> 19.6 vs 24.8 pp)" collapses when biology-helper-with-physics-primary
> (+48 pp) is contrasted with math-helper-with-physics-primary
> (+42 pp). Variance decomposition (§6m) attributes 12.9% of total
> variance to interaction vs only 3.8% to helper main effect,
> consistent with this picture.

### Bootstrap CI to include

Update the headline sentence to include the CI:

> *...the spread of gain from collaboration across primaries is 34.0
> pp, while the spread across helpers is 7.6 pp — a primary-effect-
> to-helper-effect ratio of **4.47× (95% CI 1.89–8.39, 1000-iteration
> within-cell bootstrap; question-clustered bootstrap pending audit
> follow-up #9)**.*

The CI is wide enough that "5×" is *not* a defensible round-number
shorthand; "approximately 4×" is fine; "between 2× and 8×" is the
honest envelope that survives uncertainty.

**Three paper sentences that should NOT yet appear in the abstract**
(per claim_evidence_map.md gap items 5–8):

- "13.5× better switching quality" (deprecated count-ratio claim
  whose denominator was not normalized)
- "rank constraint destroys collaboration" (no matched-solo-accuracy
  comparison yet exists)
- "scales from 1.7B to 4B" (only 4B Full FT bio/law/math + base
  exist; 4B LoRA at matched N is missing)

The abstract should go through one more revision pass before the
2026-05-05 audit findings are bedded in. Audit follow-ups #4, #5, #7
are the gates.

Mapping each likely concern to the audit section that addresses it,
and to where the paper-as-currently-drafted is weakest.

| Reviewer flavor | Concern | Audit section | Status |

| Reviewer B (PEFT mechanic) | "How do you know the rank constraint is the cause and not training-data quality?" | §1, §6a-bis | **partially answered** — verified-LoRA roster, but paired 1.7B FT pair-grid not yet run (#5) |
| Reviewer C (multi-agent) | "Du et al. saw +10pp; you see deltas all over -10 to +30. Why is your range so much wider?" | §6a, §6e | answered — heterogeneous specialist+base pairs, format-matching specialists, helper-vs-base spread |
| Reviewer D (calibration) | "C2W and W2C are dominated by the base rate of 'started correct'; the count ratios are not behavioural claims." | §6a | **fully answered** in this audit — the count-ratio framing has been deprecated and replaced with conditional rates |
| Reviewer E (domain distance) | "Does cross-domain helper hurt because of distance or because of conflicting expertise?" | §6e | partially answered — base helper hurts more than any specialist; specialist-vs-specialist spread small |
| Reviewer F (statistician) | "Where are your CIs? n=50 per cell is small." | §6f, §6o | answered as of this audit — clustered 95% CI [2.20, 7.45]; per-cell Wilson disclosure 17/30 sig |
| Reviewer G (replication) | "Is this Qwen3-only, or does it hold for Llama / Mistral / etc.?" | n/a | **NOT answered** — single-base-model limitation; flag in limitations section |
| Reviewer H (reviewer 2) | "The law specialist looks like an artifact." | §6a-bis, §6c | answered — reported with and without law; same direction either way |
| Reviewer I (subject-mix) | "Your 50-question MMLU sample is dominated by easy subjects (e.g. elementary_math, high_school_biology). Is the WHO-asymmetry an artifact of subject-mix-within-primary?" | §6n, §6qq, §6rr | **fully answered** as of §6rr — subject-stratified ANOVA gives subject/helper ratio 21.7× vs primary/helper 22.1× (essentially identical). Within-primary subject heterogeneity is real (15–22 pp spread for math/medicine/physics) but does NOT dilute the row-factor-dominates-helper-factor headline; biology's row mean is uniquely homogeneous (0.5 pp spread) |
| Reviewer J (interaction) | "Helper main effect is small (3.8% var). Is your headline really about a primary-only effect, or is the interaction term doing the work?" | §6m, §6p | **answered** — interaction is 12.9% of var (vs 3.8% helper), helper-primary pairing matters more than helper identity; §6p shows within-helper col_std 9–21 pp ≫ col-mean spread 7.6 pp |
| Reviewer K (difficulty) | "Your WHO ratio is computed on a mix of easy and hard questions. Is the asymmetry driven by ceiling effects on easy questions or by genuine primary-side processing differences?" | §6cc | **answered** — easy-question WHO ratio is 1.50× (primary 24% / helper 16%, comparable); hard-question WHO ratio is 67.69× (primary 88.4%). The asymmetry is concentrated on hard questions, *not* a ceiling artifact on easy ones |
| Reviewer L (cell-role) | "Is helper-as-corrector the typical case, or is the headline driven by a few outlier cells?" | §6bb | **answered** — 24/30 cells (80%) are corrector cells; the 6 non-corrector cells are all in the law primary's row. Every helper has 4/5 corrector + 1/5 non-corrector pattern, with the non-corrector cell always being law primary |

The two reviewer concerns the paper *cannot yet* answer:

1. **Reviewer B's "rank-constraint causation"**: requires the 1.7B
   Full FT pair-grid at matched solo accuracy (audit follow-ups
   #4, #3, #5). Until this lands, the paper claims at most
   *correlation* between rank-constrained adaptation and the
   WHO-asymmetry, not *causation*.

2. **Reviewer G's "single-model"**: requires re-running on Llama
   or Mistral. Out of scope for the current submission window;
   should be in limitations / future work.

3. **Reviewer I's "subject-mix"** (status: **fully answered** as of
   §6rr). Within-primary subject heterogeneity at the mutual-
   unrecoverability level is large (math 20–75%, §6qq), but the
   formal subject-stratified WHO ratio (§6rr) gives subject/helper =
   21.7× vs primary/helper = 22.1× (essentially identical). Helper
   variance is 3% in either decomposition; row-factor variance is
   72–83%. The "primary identity dominates helper identity" finding
   can be restated as "subject identity dominates helper identity"
   with no loss of force.

The other concerns are answered or partially answered, and the
audit's §10 abstract directive bakes the answers into the
defensible-sentence list.

## 12. Audit follow-up #9: question-clustered bootstrap — RESOLVED

**Original premise was wrong.** The audit-as-written claimed that
`matrix_results.json` did not preserve question identity across cells
because per_q records used independent `idx` 0..49 with no cross-cell
join key. On inspection of `run_verified_pair_grid.py` lines 219-223,
the question pool *is* loaded once per primary (deterministic seed=42)
and reused across all 6 helper conditions — see this verification:

```python
# proof: pair_math_base.per_q[i].subject == pair_math_*.per_q[i].subject
#        for all i and all helpers
```

**Resolution (committed in this deepening pass).**

1. **Patched `run_verified_pair_grid.py`** to emit an explicit `qid`
   field on per_q records (SHA1 of subject + question text + answer,
   16-hex-char prefix). This makes the cross-cell join future-proof.

2. **Wrote `src/scripts/clustered_bootstrap_who.py`** which performs
   the question-clustered bootstrap on existing data using `(primary,
   idx)` as the join key. The script verifies cross-cell alignment
   by asserting subject and expected-answer equality before resampling.

3. **Result: 95% CI [2.20, 7.45]** — narrower than within-cell
   [1.89, 8.39], not wider. The §6f as-written prediction "clustering
   would widen the CI" was empirically falsified. The clustered CI
   is the correct uncertainty estimate (within-cell over-estimates
   on both ends because it allows cell-level deltas to drift
   independently); the lower bound shifts up to 2.20, with P(ratio
   > 2) = 99.1%. **The WHO-asymmetry headline is materially stronger
   under proper clustering**, not weaker.

§6f has been revised in-place above with the new headline CI and
the prediction-error explanation. The follow-up #9 entry in
`tasks/queue.yaml` should be marked complete.

## 13. Audit closure summary (post-§6aaa, structural exhaustion)

The 2026-05-05 audit has now exhausted its analytical surface across
all major disambiguation axes. This section catalogs what the audit
has established and what remains out of scope.

### What the audit has established

**Variance-decomposition (six-way WHO ratio family):**

| Stratification | row factor | row/helper ratio | SS_helper |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| full grid | primary (5) | 22.11× | 3.8% |
| full grid | subject (17) | 21.73× | 3.3% |
| hard subset | primary (5) | 50.34× | 1.7% |
| hard subset | subject (17) | 56.54× | 1.4% |
| easy subset | primary (5) | 1.31× | 21.8% |
| easy subset | subject (17) | 1.64× | 24.3% |

Row-factor-choice moves the ratio ≤14% relative; difficulty regime
moves it 30–40×. Helper variance only matters on easy questions.

**Replicate-aware ANOVA F-stat triple:** F_primary = 26.55 / 31.22
/ 3.13 (full / hard / easy, p < 1e-21 / 1e-23 / 0.0149). F_helper =
0.96 / 0.50 / 1.91 — non-rejecting at every stratification, with
the easy 1.91 (p = 0.092) the strongest helper signal.

**Three-level Bonferroni hierarchy at n_iter=50000:**

| Aggregation | n pairs | Bonferroni survivors |
|---|---:|---:|
| subject (§6tt, §6ww) | 136 | 11 |
| primary (§6kk, §6yy) | 10 | 2 |
| helper (§6kk, §6yy) | 15 | 0 |

Helper-pair Bonferroni is **0 of 15 at any iteration count** — the
audit's strongest formal "no helper effect" finding.

**Four-axis jackknife family:**

| Axis | n LOO | Range | Factor | Max-leverage |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| §6y full primary | 5 | 10.37–31.33× | 3.0× | law −11.74 |
| §6gg hard primary | 5 | 15.58–177.14× | 11.4× | medicine +109.45 |
| §6zz hard subject | 17 | 25.87–85.72× | 3.3× | prof_law −30.68 |
| §6aaa hard helper | 6 | 46.11–364.47× | 7.9× | base +307.93 |
| §6aaa specialists only | 5 | 46.11–57.25× | 1.24× | biology −10.43 |

All drops keep ratio above 10×; specialist-only LOO range (1.24×)
is the audit's tightest jackknife.

**Mechanistic mechanism (§6hh, §6pp, §6qq):**
- Per-primary mean W2C (recovery from solo wrong) ranges 21% (math)
  to 64% (biology). Per-helper W2C is flat at 35–41%. Primary
  spread / helper spread = 7.07×.
- 47.7% of hard questions are nearly unrecoverable (≤1 of 6 helpers);
  math/law primaries 53% mutually unrecoverable, biology 19%.
- Within math primary, mutual-unrec spans 20% (elementary_math) to
  75% (high_school_math, college_math).

**Per-subject CIs (§6tt n=2k, §6ww n=50k):**
- hs_biology W2C [50.8, 81.8] (n=22) does not overlap college_math
  [0, 12.5] (n=4) — 39 pp robust gap.
- 11 of 136 subject-pair contrasts pass Bonferroni-136 at n=50k.
- Within math primary, college_math < elementary_math by 34.2 pp
  (Bonferroni-136-significant).

### What the audit cannot establish (out of scope or queued)

- **4B Full FT pair-grid** at matched solo accuracy is partial
  (3 of 5 domains; only +base helper). The matched-FT comparison
  required for the "rank-constraint causes" causal claim is not yet
  run.
- **1.7B Full FT pair-grid** is blocked on training the missing
  law-domain Full FT specialist. *Specific blocker chain (verified
  2026-05-06):* the 5-domain sequential queue
  (medicine → math → biology → law → physics) ran 4 of 5 then
  truncated on the law tar transfer.
  `/media/dan/WD_BLACK/models/halulujah_a5000_ft_5domain/full_ft_5domain/full_ft_law.tar.gz`
  is **134 MB** (vs 2.73 GB for medicine and physics tars); `tar tzf`
  reports `unexpected end of file`. Root cause: RunPod container volume
  was undersized for the 5-domain queue (peak ~110 GB needed: 5
  domains × ~3.4 GB/checkpoint × ~4 ckpts = 68 GB + 28 GB HF cache +
  1.3 GB src + ~14 GB tar staging buffer; default container ~50 GB).
  The law tarball corruption is the smoking gun — quota was hit
  mid-write. *Unblock steps:* (a) increase RunPod container volume
  to ≥150 GB OR (b) wire streaming-cleanup queue runner using
  `development/scripts/full_ft_checkpoint_mover.py` (commit 7117baa);
  (c) require GPU-access-granted message per CLAUDE.md before
  re-launching; (d) train 1.7B Full FT law on CaseHOLD (~1h45m at A40
  1.21 it/s); (e) run matched-solo-accuracy 5-domain Full FT
  pair-grid (~9h).
- **CKA / weight-space mechanism measurement** was OOM'd on RunPod;
  paper relies on cited theory (Shuttleworth et al.) for mechanism.
- **7B scale test** is queued for post-submission; falsification
  target listed in `tasks/intuition.md`.
- **Replication on Llama / Mistral** is queued for limitations
  section (single-base-model caveat).

### Final claim status

The audit defends the WHO-asymmetry headline at every statistical
lens it applies (32 row-effect tests, 8 helper-effect lenses).
What it does NOT defend:
- Causal ("rank constraint causes") — requires matched-FT comparison
- Cross-architecture generalization — requires Llama/Mistral runs
- Weight-space mechanism — requires CKA / SVD on adapter weights

The honest paper claim status: **correlative, single-base-model, 1.7B
LoRA only, with a strong inferential and structural backbone but
without a causal layer or cross-architecture replication.**

To reproduce the audit's full evidence base from the
`matrix_results.json` source: run each script in its `src/scripts/`
location with no arguments. Most complete in <1 minute; the n_iter=50000
bootstraps (`subject_w2c_hard_bootstrap_hin.py`,
`net_corrector_bootstrap_hin.py`) complete in ~1 second each due to
vectorized batch sampling.

To verify the audit document hasn't drifted from the underlying data:
```bash
python3 -m src.scripts.verify_audit_headlines
```
Loads each JSON cited by §10 11th-revision and confirms the audit's
30 headline numbers match within tolerance. Last verified 2026-05-06:
**30 of 30 pass** ✓ (output snapshot below).

```
[ANOVA F-stat triple]                      6 of 6 pass
[Variance ratio family]                    6 of 6 pass
[Bonferroni hierarchy: n=50k bootstrap]    3 of 3 pass (subj 11, prim 2, help 0)
[Bonferroni hierarchy: z-test]             3 of 3 pass (subj 28, help 0, uncorr 0)
[Per-primary mean W2C on hard]             3 of 3 pass (math 21%, bio 64%, law 21%)
[Mutually-unrecoverable rate]              1 of 1 pass (47.7%)
[Per-subject hard W2C 95% CI]              4 of 4 pass (hs_bio + college_math bounds)
[8-pair ironclad intersection (§6ccc)]     4 of 4 pass (size 8/3/20 + enumeration)
```

The 8-pair enumeration check was added 2026-05-06 after a labeling
drift was caught (commit c34fcec): the prior §6ccc revision listed
`college_med > prof_law` as #6 in the intersection, but that pair
is bootstrap-only. The verification script now compares the
intersection set to the audit's claimed 8 pairs and prints a diff
on mismatch. Future similar drift would be surfaced immediately.
